FERRET FRENZY
Eight-Role d6/d12 Mobile Social-Deduction Design Dossier
Revision 2.0
Prepared October 2026

DOCUMENT PURPOSE

This revision supersedes the earlier Ferret Frenzy dossier wherever the two documents conflict. It deliberately collapses the role roster to exactly eight named roles while retaining a twenty-four-card physical/digital deck pool. It also replaces the older six-hour wake system with a twelve-hour private wake system driven by a d12 and formalizes the handheld control scheme around a joystick, numbered buttons, and a separate d6 action die.

The eight playable role names are, and only are:

1. Bandit
2. Dooker
3. Itchy
4. Trouble
5. Business
6. Snuggler
7. Hunter
8. Guardian

There is no Tanner role, no Sheriff, no Mayor, no leader role, no police analogue, no separate Robber, no separate Thief, and no Black Mitt role. Ferrets in this game are not organized into a civic hierarchy. They are a mischievous business of ferrets trying to work out who made off with the Minnow Treats while several of them have been moving cards, waking at strange hours, bonding with one another, or changing allegiance.

The twenty-four-card pool is fixed at:

4 x Bandit
14 x Business
1 x Dooker
1 x Itchy
1 x Trouble
1 x Snuggler
1 x Hunter
1 x Guardian

This gives the game only eight named role types while preserving a deck large enough for different player-count recipes, three-card Hammock reserves, and repeated rematches without making every ferret a special role.

PART I - RESEARCH FOUNDATION

1. WHY THIS REVISION USES A ONE-NIGHT STRUCTURE

The most important structural decision is to use one chaotic night followed by one morning discussion and one simultaneous Paw Point vote. This is closer to the strengths of Cheese Thief and One Night Ultimate Werewolf than to a long elimination campaign.

Cheese Thief contributes three especially useful ideas. First, each player has private wake-time information. Second, a player who wakes alone can gain additional evidence. Third, the thief must perform the theft when their wake time is called, so the theft becomes part of the evidence timeline rather than a free-floating hidden action. The official Cheese Thief rules also add Followers whose allegiance changes, which directly informs Trouble's accomplice conversion and the broader idea that a player can begin the night trustworthy and end it helping the thief.

One Night Ultimate Werewolf contributes card-state uncertainty. The Seer gains information, the Witch can view a center card and exchange it with a player's card, and the Troublemaker exchanges two other players' cards without viewing them. Those distinctions are retained rather than blended together indiscriminately. Dooker therefore uses informed card interference, while Trouble uses blind card interference.

Ultimate Werewolf's larger role library contributes the ingredients for Hunter and Guardian. Masons show how two players can begin with mutual recognition. The Cursed shows how a player can begin aligned with the majority but later convert to the hostile side. The classic Hunter supplies a delayed retaliation concept. The Chupacabra supplies the idea of a hunter whose dangerous effect is constrained to wolves first rather than freely eliminating innocents. Bodyguard and One Night Daybreak's Bodyguard provide two different protection models; Ferret Frenzy uses a one-use vote shield because the default game has no nightly player-kill phase.

Research references used for this revision include:
- Ultimate Werewolf: Ultimate Edition rules and role descriptions.
- One Night Ultimate Werewolf and One Night Ultimate Werewolf: Daybreak role rules and wake order.
- Cheese Thief English rules, especially wake-time dice, solo peeking, theft timing, Followers, discussion, and final voting.
- American Ferret Association color and pattern standards.
- PetMD's ferret color, marking, and pattern overview.

This dossier uses those systems as research inspirations, but all wording, UI, role names, interactions, dice architecture, fictional setting, and exact implementation rules below are Ferret Frenzy's own design.

2. FERRET TERMINOLOGY AND VISUAL ACCURACY

The American Ferret Association distinguishes color from pattern. Color describes guard hair, undercoat, eyes, and nose. Pattern describes concentration and distribution of color, including roaning and white markings such as mitts and blaze markings. PetMD similarly distinguishes standard colors from markings and patterns and notes that coat appearance does not determine personality or care needs.

That distinction matters here because the role labels are game roles, while the visual assignments are ferret appearance references. The game must never imply that a Cinnamon ferret is naturally inattentive, a Silver Mitt naturally sees more, a Blaze is naturally a hunter, or a Dark-Eyed White is naturally protective. Those associations are fictional casting choices for card readability.

Final visual casting:
- Bandit: Sable / Black Sable family.
- Dooker: Silver Mitt, art-directed as a Black Roan Mitt / Silver with very obvious white mitts and bib.
- Itchy: Cinnamon.
- Trouble: Champagne.
- Business: Roan, art-directed as a warm Sable Roan without prominent mitt markings.
- Snuggler: Chocolate.
- Hunter: Blaze.
- Guardian: Dark-Eyed White.

The Dooker and Business distinction requires particular care. The AFA uses Black Roan as a Silver category, and roan can occur over multiple base colors. Dooker therefore needs a cool black-and-white salt-and-pepper coat with clear white feet and bib. Business should use a warmer sable-based roan with colored hairs evenly mixed with white hairs and no large white mitt emphasis. At card size, one should read as cool silver with white paws; the other should read as warm roan.

PART II - CORE GAME IDENTITY

3. HIGH CONCEPT

Ferret Frenzy is a mobile-first hidden-role deduction game about a business of ferrets waking at different points during a twelve-hour Burrow Clock. One or more Bandits are trying to steal a bag of prized Minnow Treats and survive the morning accusation. Everybody else is trying to reconstruct the night from private wake times, who they saw awake, whether the treats were still present, card movement, partial clues, bonds, and uncertain loyalties.

The tone is mischievous rather than punitive. Nobody is a sheriff, mayor, cop, judge, or village leader. Nobody is "executed." Ferrets are caught, revealed, discovered with suspicious crumbs, dragged into the discussion circle, or sent to the hammock for the remainder of the round. The game treats the players as peers.

A round has five broad stages:
1. Build the active card set and deal starting roles.
2. Privately roll wake hours and any role-specific d6 checks.
3. Resolve the twelve Burrow Hours and role actions.
4. Hold Morning Business discussion.
5. Cast one simultaneous Paw Point vote and resolve the result.

The app is the moderator. All dice are rolled on the player's own private screen. Other players never see raw die results during the round unless a role explicitly reveals information about them.

4. THE MINNOW TREATS

The central object is the Minnow Treats. In fiction, this is a sealed bag or tin of tiny fish-based ferret treats stored in the shared stash. The treats are a game prop, not feeding advice. The app should not imply quantities, feeding schedules, or nutritional recommendations.

At the beginning of every round, the Minnow Treats are PRESENT.

At the Raider's theft hour, the treats become MISSING.

Every player who wakes after that theft sees MISSING. A player who wakes before the theft sees PRESENT. Players awake during the theft window may witness additional information depending on their role and the exact event order.

The Treat State is one of the game's strongest pieces of medium-strength evidence. It narrows the theft window without directly proving role identity.

PART III - DICE AND PHYSICAL-CONTROL MODEL

5. TWO-DIE SYSTEM

Ferret Frenzy uses exactly two mechanical dice:

d6 = action/chance die.
d12 = wake-hour die.

The dice do different jobs and should never be treated as interchangeable.

The d12 chooses one of twelve Burrow Hours. A player whose role normally wakes once rolls one d12. A role that wakes more than once rolls multiple d12s. Multiple d12s belonging to the same player must resolve to distinct hours; if a duplicate appears, only the duplicate is rerolled until all of that player's wake hours are unique.

The d6 handles chance checks, conditional conversions, and rare tie procedures that are part of a role's rules. It never decides a normal wake hour.

All rolls are private to the user who rolled them. The authoritative game state may store the result so the app can resolve rules correctly, but another player's client never receives the raw value unless a rule explicitly grants that information.

After the round, a host option called Trail of Trouble may reveal the complete dice history. The default replay reveals enough to explain the result, but groups may choose whether exact private rolls remain hidden even after the game.

6. CONTROLLER / SHELL INPUTS

The required control concept is:

JOYSTICK
- Navigate menus and highlighted targets.
- Flick the joystick while a die is selected to roll that die.
- The roll begins on a deliberate flick, not on ordinary navigation.

NUMBER BUTTON /1/
- Select d6.
- The d6 becomes the active die for the next joystick-flick roll.

NUMBER BUTTON /2/
- Select d12.
- The d12 becomes the active die for the next joystick-flick roll.

NUMBER BUTTON /3/
- Optional quick-confirm / lock result shortcut.
- This button may be hidden in simplified layouts because the dice result itself is authoritative once the animation completes.

NUMBER BUTTON /4/
- Optional private-info shortcut.
- Opens the player's role, wake schedule, observed Treat States, co-wakers, and confirmed private facts.

A
- Primary confirm / select.

B
- Back / close / cancel before an action is locked.

X
- Context-sensitive role action when the current role has one.

Y
- Private notebook / reminder screen.

Z and R
- Reserved. They are not required by the core rules and may be omitted from the Ferret Frenzy shell to reduce clutter.

The minimum functional control set is therefore joystick + /1/ + /2/ + A + B. The larger shell may retain additional buttons, but no core rule should depend on a button that is absent from the reduced configuration.

7. PRIVATE ROLL UX

Every roll follows the same privacy sequence.

1. The game tells the player which die is required.
2. The matching die selector highlights.
3. The player presses /1/ for d6 or /2/ for d12.
4. A small private banner confirms the selected die.
5. The player flicks the joystick.
6. The die rolls in the player's viewport.
7. The result locks locally and is sent to the authoritative match state.
8. The player receives only information they are entitled to know.

The result should not appear on the shared host display. A remote player's device should not receive another player's die animation, face value, random seed, or timing. Network packets may necessarily represent state changes, but the client-facing game protocol should expose only derived events such as WAKE_AT_HOUR_7 or HUNTER_CONVERTED to the player entitled to that information.

No role may force another player to physically hand over their device. When a rule says "inspect a wake hour," the app reveals only the permitted derived value on the inspecting player's own screen.

8. BURROW CLOCK: TWELVE WAKE WINDOWS

The Burrow Clock has twelve numbered wake windows, 1 through 12. These are game-hours, not literal real-world clock times. This avoids implying that Hour 10 must mean 10 a.m. or that a ferret's real sleep schedule follows the game's fiction.

The shared display may depict the twelve positions as a clock face. The number rolled on a d12 maps directly to the corresponding position.

The host sequence advances:
Hour 1
Hour 2
Hour 3
...
Hour 12
Morning Business

When an hour opens, only clients belonging to players assigned to that hour receive the WAKE event. They see:
- the Treat State, PRESENT or MISSING;
- the identities/avatars of other players awake in that same window;
- any role-specific action prompt;
- any special event, such as witnessing the Raider or triggering Hunter's conversion check.

Players not assigned to that hour remain in Night Lock and receive no information about who is currently awake.

9. WHY d12 INSTEAD OF CHEESE THIEF'S d6

Cheese Thief's six wake windows work elegantly for its intended player counts, but Ferret Frenzy gives several roles multiple wake moments and includes card interference. A d12 creates twice as many possible time slots, reducing constant crowding and making each collision more meaningful.

With a single d12 per player, any two independently rolled wake hours match with probability 1/12. Hunter rolls two distinct d12s, so against one independently timed Bandit the chance that at least one Hunter wake matches that Bandit's hour is about sixteen percent before the d6 conversion check. If Hunter only converts on d6 results 1-2, the combined conversion chance against one Bandit is a little over five percent under simple independent assumptions. Multiple Bandits increase the overlap opportunity.

Those numbers are intentionally modest. Hunter's betrayal should be a memorable possibility, not the expected state every round.

Dooker is intentionally more active. Three unique d12 wake hours give Dooker more opportunities to witness other ferrets, but Dooker's hard-information and card-swap powers remain limited to one use each. Extra wake windows therefore create social evidence without turning Dooker into an omniscient role.

PART IV - CARD POOL AND MATCH SETUP

10. FIXED 24-CARD POOL

The entire Ferret Frenzy card library for the base game is:

Bandit x4
Business x14
Dooker x1
Itchy x1
Trouble x1
Snuggler x1
Hunter x1
Guardian x1

Total: 24 cards.

There are no other base-game roles.

The deck is a pool, not a statement that twenty-four people must play at once. The recommended mobile experience is 4-10 players, with 4-12 supported after testing. The d12 wake architecture makes larger lobbies technically workable, but multi-wake roles increase effective occupancy of the clock. Twenty-four simultaneous players would produce too many collisions for the evidence style the game is built around.

For a one-night match, the host chooses or generates an ACTIVE SET containing one card per player plus three Hammock cards. The remaining cards stay outside the round.

The three Hammock cards matter because Dooker can inspect and introduce one of them. They also allow the game to contain uncertainty about how many Bandit cards are actually in players' hands.

11. ROLE DEAL AND CARD STATE

Every player begins with one STARTING CARD.

Three cards are placed in the virtual HAMMOCK.

A player's night action is determined by the role they started with unless the role's own text says otherwise. This mirrors the useful logic of one-night card-moving games: a player can perform an action and later discover that their card was moved.

The app separately tracks:
- Starting Role.
- Current Card.
- Current Allegiance.
- Raider Status.
- Trouble Accomplice Status.
- Hunter Conversion Status.
- Bond Status.
- Guardian Protection.
- Hunt Mark.

These fields must not be collapsed into one generic "role" variable because they answer different questions.

The Raider tag is historical and player-bound. Once a player actually steals the Minnow Treats, moving their card later does not erase the fact that they were the Raider.

Trouble's accomplice status is also player-bound.

Hunter's conversion status is player-bound.

Snuggler's bond is player-bound.

This means a card swap can alter final role information and team logic without rewriting what physically happened earlier in the night.

12. BANDIT COUNT AND RAIDER SELECTION

The active recipe determines how many Bandit cards are in the Active Set.

Recommended starting points:
4-5 players: usually 1 Bandit in the Active Set.
6-7 players: usually 1-2 Bandits.
8-10 players: usually 2 Bandits.
11-12 players: usually 2-3 Bandits.

Not every Bandit card in the Active Set will necessarily be dealt to a player; some may be in the Hammock.

Each starting Bandit player rolls one d12.

The starting Bandit with the earliest wake hour is designated RAIDER and steals the Minnow Treats at that hour.

If two or more starting Bandits tie for the earliest d12 result, only those tied Bandits privately roll a d6. The lowest unique d6 becomes Raider. If the d6 is tied, only the tied players reroll until one is lowest.

All starting Bandits are informed of the Bandit roster and Raider identity, but they are not shown one another's raw d12 or d6 values unless their own wake observations later reveal related information.

If card movement later changes a Bandit card, the Raider tag does not move. This keeps the theft mystery coherent.

PART V - COMPLETE EIGHT-ROLE COMPENDIUM

The following eight roles are the entire base-game roster. Appearance references are visual casting, not claims about real ferret temperament.

ROLE 1: BANDIT

Visual variety: Sable / Black Sable
Mechanical lineage: Werewolf + Cheese Thief thief
Starting alignment: Bandit side
Wake dice: 1 d12
d6 use: Only for Raider tiebreaks when earliest Bandit wake hours tie.

CORE FANTASY
Starting Bandits know the Bandit roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake becomes Raider and steals the Minnow Treats. Bandits may lie about time, Treat State, and role claims at Morning Business.

INFORMATION PROFILE
Bandits know more allegiance information than ordinary ferrets but do not automatically know what Dooker, Itchy, Hunter, Guardian, or Trouble learned.

BLUFF PROFILE
A strong Bandit claim uses mostly true timing information and changes only the detail needed to protect the Raider.

BALANCE GUARDRAILS
Do not let every Bandit receive additional special powers. The hostile team's strength comes from coordination, uncertainty, and conversions.

CARD-ART DIRECTION
Warm deep-brown Sable through cool ash-blackish Black Sable. Use one shared Bandit card identity because the two dark varieties are too close for reliable small-card role separation.

MOBILE RESOLUTION NOTES

Bandit always receives a private action card that distinguishes FACT from CHOICE. Facts are server-confirmed information such as a wake hour, a Treat State, a co-waker identity, a successful conversion, or a card the role was explicitly allowed to view. Choices are player inputs such as a swap target, bond target, protection target, or Hunt Mark. The app must never display a deduction as though it were a confirmed fact.

When a card is moved, the post-game replay records the position before and after movement. When an allegiance-changing status is applied, the status is recorded separately from card identity. This is essential for Bandit because Ferret Frenzy intentionally allows a player's starting role, current card, and allegiance status to tell different parts of the night's story.

ACCESSIBILITY

Every Bandit action has a text label, screen-reader label, and non-audio confirmation. Haptics may reinforce wake windows or action-ready moments but can never be the only information channel. Role colors are supported by names, icons, and coat-pattern silhouettes rather than color alone.

ROLE 2: DOOKER

Visual variety: Silver Mitt / Black Roan Mitt
Mechanical lineage: Seer + Daybreak Witch + frequent Cheese Thief-style waking
Starting alignment: Business side unless final allegiance changes through card state
Wake dice: 3 distinct d12s
d6 use: No routine d6 check.

CORE FANTASY
Chronological first wake: ordinary observation. Second wake: one Glimpse, learning Bandit-aligned or Not Bandit-aligned for one chosen player at that moment. Third wake: one Trip, view one Hammock card and optionally exchange it with any player's current card without viewing the card taken from that player.

INFORMATION PROFILE
Dooker sees three Treat States/co-waker sets and receives one hard-but-binary alignment glimpse.

BLUFF PROFILE
Dooker can withhold which wake contained the Glimpse or Trip, creating uncertainty without fabricating extra information.

BALANCE GUARDRAILS
Three wakes are strong. Limit to exactly one Glimpse and one Trip, with no full exact-role scan.

CARD-ART DIRECTION
Cool salt-and-pepper Silver with conspicuous white mitts and bib. Keep the paws bright and the overall coat cooler than Business.

MOBILE RESOLUTION NOTES

Dooker always receives a private action card that distinguishes FACT from CHOICE. Facts are server-confirmed information such as a wake hour, a Treat State, a co-waker identity, a successful conversion, or a card the role was explicitly allowed to view. Choices are player inputs such as a swap target, bond target, protection target, or Hunt Mark. The app must never display a deduction as though it were a confirmed fact.

When a card is moved, the post-game replay records the position before and after movement. When an allegiance-changing status is applied, the status is recorded separately from card identity. This is essential for Dooker because Ferret Frenzy intentionally allows a player's starting role, current card, and allegiance status to tell different parts of the night's story.

ACCESSIBILITY

Every Dooker action has a text label, screen-reader label, and non-audio confirmation. Haptics may reinforce wake windows or action-ready moments but can never be the only information channel. Role colors are supported by names, icons, and coat-pattern silhouettes rather than color alone.

ROLE 3: ITCHY

Visual variety: Cinnamon
Mechanical lineage: Little Girl / partial observer translated into accessible digital clues
Starting alignment: Business side unless final allegiance changes
Wake dice: Normally 1 d12; on an activated observation night, 2 distinct d12s
d6 use: At setup roll d6. On 1-4, Itchy is too distracted and receives no special clue. On 5-6, Itchy gets a second d12 wake and one partial Bandit clue.

CORE FANTASY
When activated, the app gives a deliberately incomplete clue after the raid: two player identities are shown, exactly one of whom is the Raider when the rules can generate a valid decoy. Itchy is never told which one.

INFORMATION PROFILE
Itchy always knows their normal wake evidence and only sometimes receives a role-specific clue.

BLUFF PROFILE
Because everyone knows Itchy may fail to notice anything, a claimed no-clue night is plausible but not self-proving.

BALANCE GUARDRAILS
The clue activates only one third of nights by default and must remain partial. Never reveal an exact Bandit identity.

CARD-ART DIRECTION
Rich light reddish-brown guard hairs with a visible warm red cast. The 'itchy' behavior is fictional character comedy, not a color-linked trait.

MOBILE RESOLUTION NOTES

Itchy always receives a private action card that distinguishes FACT from CHOICE. Facts are server-confirmed information such as a wake hour, a Treat State, a co-waker identity, a successful conversion, or a card the role was explicitly allowed to view. Choices are player inputs such as a swap target, bond target, protection target, or Hunt Mark. The app must never display a deduction as though it were a confirmed fact.

When a card is moved, the post-game replay records the position before and after movement. When an allegiance-changing status is applied, the status is recorded separately from card identity. This is essential for Itchy because Ferret Frenzy intentionally allows a player's starting role, current card, and allegiance status to tell different parts of the night's story.

ACCESSIBILITY

Every Itchy action has a text label, screen-reader label, and non-audio confirmation. Haptics may reinforce wake windows or action-ready moments but can never be the only information channel. Role colors are supported by names, icons, and coat-pattern silhouettes rather than color alone.

ROLE 4: TROUBLE

Visual variety: Champagne
Mechanical lineage: One Night Troublemaker + Cheese Thief Follower/accomplice
Starting alignment: Begins Business-side; may convert to Bandit-side accomplice
Wake dice: 1 d12
d6 use: No routine d6 check.

CORE FANTASY
At Trouble's wake, blind-swap the current cards of two other players without seeing either card. If Trouble is awake in the same hour that the Raider steals the Minnow Treats, Trouble becomes In On It before resolving the swap.

INFORMATION PROFILE
Trouble knows exactly which two player positions were exchanged but not the card identities moved.

BLUFF PROFILE
A converted Trouble can truthfully report the swap while selectively lying about who was awake or whether the treats were present.

BALANCE GUARDRAILS
Catching Trouble alone does not count as catching the Raider. Only one Trouble card exists.

CARD-ART DIRECTION
Pale tan / diluted chocolate Champagne coat with light-to-dark burgundy eyes. Keep it much paler than Snuggler's Chocolate.

MOBILE RESOLUTION NOTES

Trouble always receives a private action card that distinguishes FACT from CHOICE. Facts are server-confirmed information such as a wake hour, a Treat State, a co-waker identity, a successful conversion, or a card the role was explicitly allowed to view. Choices are player inputs such as a swap target, bond target, protection target, or Hunt Mark. The app must never display a deduction as though it were a confirmed fact.

When a card is moved, the post-game replay records the position before and after movement. When an allegiance-changing status is applied, the status is recorded separately from card identity. This is essential for Trouble because Ferret Frenzy intentionally allows a player's starting role, current card, and allegiance status to tell different parts of the night's story.

ACCESSIBILITY

Every Trouble action has a text label, screen-reader label, and non-audio confirmation. Haptics may reinforce wake windows or action-ready moments but can never be the only information channel. Role colors are supported by names, icons, and coat-pattern silhouettes rather than color alone.

ROLE 5: BUSINESS

Visual variety: Warm Sable Roan
Mechanical lineage: Villager/Sleepyhead ordinary evidence role
Starting alignment: Business side unless final card state changes
Wake dice: 1 d12
d6 use: No routine d6 check.

CORE FANTASY
No unique special action. Business sees Treat State and co-wakers. If Business wakes alone, it may privately inspect one other player's wake hour for that round.

INFORMATION PROFILE
Business is the evidence backbone. The role has no magical or exceptional information beyond the shared wake system and solo-peek rule.

BLUFF PROFILE
Business claims are simple enough for Bandits to imitate, which is important for social deduction.

BALANCE GUARDRAILS
There are fourteen Business cards in the twenty-four-card pool so ordinary evidence remains common.

CARD-ART DIRECTION
Warm Sable Roan, with evenly mixed white guard hairs over a warm sable base. Avoid obvious white mitts so the card does not resemble Dooker.

MOBILE RESOLUTION NOTES

Business always receives a private action card that distinguishes FACT from CHOICE. Facts are server-confirmed information such as a wake hour, a Treat State, a co-waker identity, a successful conversion, or a card the role was explicitly allowed to view. Choices are player inputs such as a swap target, bond target, protection target, or Hunt Mark. The app must never display a deduction as though it were a confirmed fact.

When a card is moved, the post-game replay records the position before and after movement. When an allegiance-changing status is applied, the status is recorded separately from card identity. This is essential for Business because Ferret Frenzy intentionally allows a player's starting role, current card, and allegiance status to tell different parts of the night's story.

ACCESSIBILITY

Every Business action has a text label, screen-reader label, and non-audio confirmation. Haptics may reinforce wake windows or action-ready moments but can never be the only information channel. Role colors are supported by names, icons, and coat-pattern silhouettes rather than color alone.

ROLE 6: SNUGGLER

Visual variety: Chocolate
Mechanical lineage: Cupid / Lovers bond
Starting alignment: Retains normal allegiance; bond is a relationship, not a third faction
Wake dice: 1 d12
d6 use: No routine d6 check.

CORE FANTASY
Before Hour 1, choose two players to become Snuggle-Bonded. Those players privately learn each other's identity as bonded partners. If either bonded player is caught/revealed by the final Paw Point resolution, the other bonded player is also revealed as part of the same resolution.

INFORMATION PROFILE
Snuggler knows the bond pair. The pair know each other but not each other's role unless another rule reveals it.

BLUFF PROFILE
Odd cooperation may come from a real bond, a Bandit alliance, Guardian trust, or ordinary social play.

BALANCE GUARDRAILS
Bond follows the players, not the cards. Card swaps do not erase or move the bond.

CARD-ART DIRECTION
Warm milk-chocolate brown guard hairs, darker and richer than Champagne, with soft cream undercoat.

MOBILE RESOLUTION NOTES

Snuggler always receives a private action card that distinguishes FACT from CHOICE. Facts are server-confirmed information such as a wake hour, a Treat State, a co-waker identity, a successful conversion, or a card the role was explicitly allowed to view. Choices are player inputs such as a swap target, bond target, protection target, or Hunt Mark. The app must never display a deduction as though it were a confirmed fact.

When a card is moved, the post-game replay records the position before and after movement. When an allegiance-changing status is applied, the status is recorded separately from card identity. This is essential for Snuggler because Ferret Frenzy intentionally allows a player's starting role, current card, and allegiance status to tell different parts of the night's story.

ACCESSIBILITY

Every Snuggler action has a text label, screen-reader label, and non-audio confirmation. Haptics may reinforce wake windows or action-ready moments but can never be the only information channel. Role colors are supported by names, icons, and coat-pattern silhouettes rather than color alone.

ROLE 7: HUNTER

Visual variety: Blaze
Mechanical lineage: Mason trust + Cursed conversion + classic Hunter + Chupacabra restriction
Starting alignment: Begins paired with Guardian on Business side; may convert to Bandit side
Wake dice: 2 distinct d12s
d6 use: If either Hunter wake matches any starting Bandit's wake hour, Hunter immediately rolls d6 once for the first such overlap. On 1-2 Hunter converts to Bandit side; on 3-6 Hunter remains loyal.

CORE FANTASY
Hunter and Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. After Hunter's later chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter secretly places one Hunt Mark on a player. If Hunter is caught by the final vote while still loyal, the marked player is checked: if that player is Bandit-aligned they are also caught; if not, nothing happens. Converted Hunter loses the Hunt Mark effect.

INFORMATION PROFILE
Hunter sees two wake windows and may directly encounter Bandits. Guardian never receives conversion information.

BLUFF PROFILE
A converted Hunter can continue behaving like Guardian's trusted partner because the original trust was genuine.

BALANCE GUARDRAILS
Conversion must stay rare. Default low d6 threshold is 1-2, not 1-3. The Hunt Mark only fires if Hunter is caught and only harms a Bandit-aligned target.

CARD-ART DIRECTION
Strong Blaze pattern with a long white stripe from forehead between the ears down the neck. This gives Hunter a clean silhouette unlike the dark Bandit.

MOBILE RESOLUTION NOTES

Hunter always receives a private action card that distinguishes FACT from CHOICE. Facts are server-confirmed information such as a wake hour, a Treat State, a co-waker identity, a successful conversion, or a card the role was explicitly allowed to view. Choices are player inputs such as a swap target, bond target, protection target, or Hunt Mark. The app must never display a deduction as though it were a confirmed fact.

When a card is moved, the post-game replay records the position before and after movement. When an allegiance-changing status is applied, the status is recorded separately from card identity. This is essential for Hunter because Ferret Frenzy intentionally allows a player's starting role, current card, and allegiance status to tell different parts of the night's story.

ACCESSIBILITY

Every Hunter action has a text label, screen-reader label, and non-audio confirmation. Haptics may reinforce wake windows or action-ready moments but can never be the only information channel. Role colors are supported by names, icons, and coat-pattern silhouettes rather than color alone.

ROLE 8: GUARDIAN

Visual variety: Dark-Eyed White
Mechanical lineage: Mason trust + Bodyguard vote protection
Starting alignment: Begins Business side
Wake dice: 1 d12
d6 use: No routine d6 check.

CORE FANTASY
Guardian and Hunter recognize each other before Hour 1. Once per game, immediately before Paw Point locks, Guardian privately protects one other player. A protected player cannot be the sole player caught by the vote. Guardian is never told whether Hunter converted.

INFORMATION PROFILE
Guardian's most important information can become stale. They know who began as Hunter, not who Hunter is aligned with by dawn.

BLUFF PROFILE
Guardian can claim trust in Hunter without revealing whether they intend to spend protection there.

BALANCE GUARDRAILS
Guardian cannot protect self. Protection does not prevent card swaps, Snuggle bonds, role clues, or Hunter conversion. It only changes final vote capture.

CARD-ART DIRECTION
White-to-cream coat with dark/burgundy eyes and pink nose. Keep the eyes clearly dark enough to separate Guardian from an albino appearance.

MOBILE RESOLUTION NOTES

Guardian always receives a private action card that distinguishes FACT from CHOICE. Facts are server-confirmed information such as a wake hour, a Treat State, a co-waker identity, a successful conversion, or a card the role was explicitly allowed to view. Choices are player inputs such as a swap target, bond target, protection target, or Hunt Mark. The app must never display a deduction as though it were a confirmed fact.

When a card is moved, the post-game replay records the position before and after movement. When an allegiance-changing status is applied, the status is recorded separately from card identity. This is essential for Guardian because Ferret Frenzy intentionally allows a player's starting role, current card, and allegiance status to tell different parts of the night's story.

ACCESSIBILITY

Every Guardian action has a text label, screen-reader label, and non-audio confirmation. Haptics may reinforce wake windows or action-ready moments but can never be the only information channel. Role colors are supported by names, icons, and coat-pattern silhouettes rather than color alone.

PART VI - NIGHT ORDER AND EVENT RESOLUTION

13. PRE-NIGHT SETUP

The following events happen before Hour 1:

A. Deal Starting Cards and three Hammock cards.
B. Privately reveal each player's starting role.
C. Snuggler chooses the bonded pair.
D. Hunter and Guardian privately recognize each other if both are player-held starting roles.
E. Each player rolls the wake d12s required by their starting role.
F. Itchy rolls d6 to determine whether the observation night activates. If activated, Itchy rolls the extra d12.
G. Determine starting Bandit roster and Raider from the earliest Bandit d12, using private d6 tiebreaks if necessary.
H. Lock the Burrow Clock schedule.

Snuggler's bond and Hunter/Guardian recognition happen before card movement so that these relationships remain attached to people rather than to later card positions.

The Raider is determined before Hour 1 because the game must know when the Minnow Treats will be stolen.

14. ORDER WITHIN A BURROW HOUR

At each hour, resolve in this order:

1. WAKE
The correct clients wake and see the other players assigned to the hour.

2. TREAT STATE BEFORE ACTION
Players see whether the Minnow Treats are PRESENT at the beginning of the window.

3. RAIDER THEFT
If this is the Raider's hour and the treats are still present, the Raider steals them.

4. WITNESS / CONVERSION TRIGGERS
Trouble converts to In On It if present during the theft.
Hunter checks for Bandit overlap. On the first applicable overlap, Hunter rolls the private d6. A 1-2 converts Hunter; a 3-6 leaves Hunter loyal.
Itchy does not gain a full identity reveal simply by sharing the window; its special partial clue remains governed by its activation rule.

5. STARTING-ROLE ACTIONS
Dooker's Glimpse or Trip, Trouble's blind swap, Business's solo wake-hour inspection, and any other scheduled action resolve.

6. STATE COMMIT
The authoritative event log records card positions, allegiances, Treat State, and private facts.

7. SLEEP
Clients return to Night Lock.

This order matters. In particular, Trouble witnesses/converts before doing its blind swap in a theft window. Hunter's conversion check also occurs before the Hunter later decides a Hunt Mark.

15. DOOKER'S THREE WAKE WINDOWS

Dooker rolls three unique d12s. Sort them chronologically.

Earliest Dooker hour:
- ordinary observation only;
- sees co-wakers and Treat State.

Middle Dooker hour:
- may use GLIMPSE once;
- choose one player;
- learn BANDIT-ALIGNED or NOT BANDIT-ALIGNED at that exact moment;
- do not learn exact role.

Latest Dooker hour:
- may use TRIP once;
- view exactly one Hammock card;
- then optionally exchange that Hammock card with any one player's current card;
- Dooker does not view the card removed from that player as part of the Trip action.

The player may pass either special action. Passing is private.

This order intentionally limits Dooker. Extra waking creates more observation, while only one wake produces hard alignment information and only one wake produces informed card movement.

16. ITCHY'S PARTIAL-OBSERVATION CHECK

At setup, Itchy selects d6 with /1/ and flicks the joystick.

Results 1-4:
TOO ITCHY.
Itchy receives no special observation this round and rolls one d12 wake hour.

Results 5-6:
EARS UP.
Itchy rolls two distinct d12 wake hours instead of one. After the Raider theft, the app prepares a partial clue. The preferred clue format is a two-player pair containing the Raider and one eligible non-Raider decoy. Itchy sees both names/avatars but is not told which one is the Raider.

If no legal decoy exists, the app substitutes a timing clue rather than revealing the Raider directly, for example:
"The Raider woke in Hours 1-6"
or
"The Raider woke in Hours 7-12."

The partial clue is delivered privately after Itchy's later wake or at the end of Hour 12, whichever is clearer for the implementation.

The d6 outcome itself remains private.

17. HUNTER CONVERSION AND HUNT MARK

Hunter rolls two unique d12 wake hours.

If Hunter shares either hour with any starting Bandit player, the first overlap triggers a private d6 roll.

d6 1-2:
Hunter converts to the Bandit side.

d6 3-6:
Hunter remains loyal to the Business side.

The conversion result is shown only to Hunter and the authoritative state. Guardian receives no alert, no haptic, no icon change, and no indirect UI clue.

If Hunter is still loyal after the later of their two wake hours, Hunter secretly chooses one Hunt Mark target.

The Hunt Mark is intentionally delayed and conditional:
- If Hunter is not caught by the final vote, the mark does nothing.
- If Hunter is caught and remained loyal, inspect the marked target.
- If the marked target is Bandit-aligned at resolution, that target is also caught/revealed.
- If the marked target is not Bandit-aligned, nothing happens to them.
- If Hunter converted, the Hunt Mark is disabled.

This is the base game's Chupacabra/Hunter blend. It keeps the classic Hunter's consequence-on-capture shape while borrowing Chupacabra's rule that the dangerous effect only successfully hits the wolf-equivalent target.

18. GUARDIAN PROTECTION

Guardian's single protection is used after Morning Business but before the vote is revealed.

Guardian selects one other player.

The protected player cannot be the sole player caught by the Paw Point vote.

Resolution examples:

Example A:
Hunter receives 5 votes, everyone else receives fewer, and Guardian protected Hunter.
Hunter is protected. Move to the next-highest eligible vote total. If a single eligible player leads there, that player is caught.

Example B:
Hunter and Bandit tie at 4 votes each, and Guardian protected Hunter.
Bandit remains an eligible top-vote target and is caught/revealed. Hunter is not caught through the vote.

Example C:
Guardian protects a secretly converted Hunter.
Protection still works. Guardian does not receive any indication that the protected player is now helping the Bandits.

Guardian protection does not stop Dooker or Trouble from moving a card, does not undo Snuggler's bond, and does not prevent Hunter conversion. It is a vote-resolution shield only.

PART VII - MORNING BUSINESS, VOTING, AND WIN CONDITIONS

19. MORNING BUSINESS

After Hour 12, every client enters Morning Business.

The shared screen shows that the Minnow Treats are missing.

Players may discuss:
- claimed wake hours;
- who woke together;
- when the treats were present or missing;
- Dooker claims;
- Itchy claims;
- Trouble swap claims;
- Guardian/Hunter trust claims;
- Snuggler bond claims;
- suspected card movement;
- suspected conversions.

No app-generated suspicion score is provided. The genre works because humans combine facts, lies, omissions, and social judgment.

The app may provide a private notebook showing only confirmed personal facts. It may also provide an optional shared timeline containing public claims entered by players, but claims must be visually marked CLAIMED, never VERIFIED.

20. PAW POINT VOTE

Every active player selects one player.

Votes lock simultaneously.

There is no Sheriff, Mayor, Captain, leader, or tie-break authority.

If one unprotected player has the highest vote total, that player is caught.

If multiple unprotected players tie for the highest vote total, all tied top players are caught/revealed. This follows the useful Cheese Thief principle that a tie can expose more than one suspect rather than delegating authority to a leader.

Guardian protection is applied before deciding which top-vote players remain eligible.

Snuggle Bond and Hunter Hunt Mark may add additional reveals after the vote capture is determined.

21. PRIMARY WIN CONDITION

The Business side's primary objective is to catch the RAIDER, meaning the player who actually stole the Minnow Treats.

The Business side wins if the Raider is among the final caught/revealed players after Guardian protection, vote ties, Snuggle Bond, and Hunter Hunt Mark have resolved.

The Bandit side wins if the Raider is not caught.

This objective remains historical even if the Raider's role card was moved after the theft.

Trouble who became In On It is an accomplice. Catching Trouble alone does not satisfy the Business win condition.

A Hunter who converted is Bandit-aligned and can help the Raider survive, but catching the converted Hunter alone does not replace catching the Raider.

This keeps the mystery centered on who actually took the Minnow Treats while still allowing card movement and allegiance changes to matter.

22. SECONDARY PERSONAL RESULTS

The result screen distinguishes:
- PRIMARY ROUND RESULT: Business caught Raider / Bandits protected Raider.
- BOND RESULT: whether both Snuggle-Bonded players escaped or were caught together.
- HUNTER RESULT: loyal, converted, Hunt Mark triggered or not.
- GUARDIAN RESULT: protected target and whether protection changed vote resolution.
- DOOKER RESULT: Glimpse target/result and Trip movement.
- TROUBLE RESULT: blind swap and accomplice conversion.
- ITCHY RESULT: activation roll and partial clue.

Secondary results do not overwrite the primary Raider-vs-Business result unless a future expansion explicitly introduces alternate win conditions. The base game stays simple.

PART VIII - MOBILE PRESENTATION AND ART DIRECTION

23. CARD READABILITY

Every role card must read correctly at phone size before the user zooms.

Required hierarchy:
1. Role name.
2. Ferret portrait.
3. Role icon.
4. One-line power summary.
5. Wake-dice count.
6. Team/status summary.
7. Expandable full rules.

The visual variety name can appear as secondary text, for example:
DOOKER
Silver Mitt

or

BUSINESS
Sable Roan

The coat reference should help players recognize art without making them memorize genetic terminology.

24. ROLE ICONS

Bandit: treat bag / dark paw.
Dooker: three small clock marks plus swap arrows.
Itchy: scratch marks plus half-open eye.
Trouble: crossing arrows plus mischievous paw.
Business: clustered paw prints.
Snuggler: two curled ferrets / linked hammock loops.
Hunter: blaze-shaped target mark.
Guardian: shield around a paw.

Icons should remain identifiable in monochrome and not rely on coat color.

25. FERRET BEHAVIOR LANGUAGE

The animation system should feel ferret-like without claiming that coat patterns create personality.

General behaviors available to every coat:
- dooking during excited play;
- war-dance-inspired hops;
- tunnel exploration;
- periscoping;
- dragging light objects;
- investigating bags and boxes;
- curling into hammocks;
- social tumbling;
- abrupt bursts of energy followed by sleep.

Dooker is shown dooking more often because that is the fictional role character's behavior, not because Silver Mitt ferrets are more vocal.

Itchy may scratch in role art, but the codex should explicitly state that persistent itching in a real ferret can be a health concern and is not a Cinnamon color trait.

Hunter's alert pose and Guardian's protective pose are role acting, not breed stereotypes.

26. DICE VISUALS

The dice viewport must keep the die entirely visible through the roll and landing animation.

Selected die state:
- /1/ highlights d6.
- /2/ highlights d12.
- only the selected die is active.

A joystick flick launches the selected die into the private dice viewport.

The final face must remain readable until the player confirms or the required delay expires.

For multi-d12 rolls, the game rolls one d12 at a time rather than throwing several simultaneously. This prevents ambiguity about which result corresponds to which wake slot and makes duplicate-hour rerolls straightforward.

Dooker sees three stored wake results.
Hunter sees two stored wake results.
Activated Itchy sees two stored wake results.
All other roles normally see one.

PART IX - BALANCE FRAMEWORK

27. INFORMATION BUDGET

Information is divided into three categories.

HARD
- Dooker's Bandit/Not Bandit Glimpse.
- Hunter's private conversion result.
- Guardian's knowledge of starting Hunter identity.
- Trouble's knowledge of exactly which positions it swapped.

MEDIUM
- exact private wake hours;
- co-waker identities;
- PRESENT/MISSING Treat State;
- Dooker's known Hammock card before Trip;
- Snuggler bond identities.

SOFT
- Itchy's two-person partial clue;
- claims made during Morning Business;
- voting behavior;
- silence, timing, or social reads.

A healthy round should contain many medium facts, a small number of hard facts, and enough soft ambiguity that Bandits can still construct plausible stories.

28. MULTI-WAKE DENSITY

For balancing purposes, count wake OCCUPANCY rather than only player count.

A normal Business contributes one occupancy.
Bandit contributes one.
Trouble contributes one.
Snuggler contributes one.
Guardian contributes one.
Dooker contributes three.
Hunter contributes two.
Itchy contributes one or two.

A seven-player game containing Dooker, Hunter, and activated Itchy can create ten or more total wake occupancies across twelve hours. That is already meaningfully denser than a seven-player game in which everyone wakes once.

The matchmaking/lobby validator should display an estimated Night Density:
LOW
STANDARD
BUSY
FRENZY

This is informational, not a quality rating. Some groups will prefer dense collisions.

29. HUNTER CONVERSION PROBABILITY

With one Bandit wake hour and two independently distributed unique Hunter wake hours, the overlap probability is approximately sixteen percent. The exact value changes slightly because Hunter's own d12s are forced unique.

With the default conversion threshold of d6 1-2, only one third of overlaps convert Hunter.

This produces a rough conversion chance in the low single digits against one Bandit before accounting for exact sampling details.

That rarity is intentional. Guardian should often be right to trust Hunter, otherwise the pair ceases to function as a Mason-like anchor. The danger comes from never being completely sure.

If playtesting shows conversion is too rare, increase overlap opportunities before increasing the d6 threshold. For example, a high-chaos preset could give the Raider two possible wake windows. Do not casually change low to 1-3 because doubling the conversion probability can strongly destabilize Guardian trust.

30. RECOMMENDED ACTIVE-SET RECIPES

These recipes list player-held plus Hammock cards. Each recipe therefore contains player count + 3 cards.

4 PLAYERS / 7 ACTIVE CARDS

2 Bandit
3 Business

1 Guardian

2 Bandit
4 Business
1 Dooker

1 Guardian

9 PLAYERS / 12 ACTIVE CARDS
2 Bandit
4 Business

2 Bandit
5 Business

These are starting points, not final tournament balance. Because three cards remain in the Hammock, the number of player-held specials and Bandits varies by deal.

PART X - COMPLETE ROLE-INTERACTION MATRIX

Every pair among the eight role types is tested below. The purpose is to catch automatic solves, ambiguous ordering, and accidental information leaks before implementation.

INTERACTION 01: BANDIT x DOOKER
Primary lens: wake-hour collision

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Bandit and Dooker can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Bandit contributes 1 d12 and the following mechanical anchor: Starting Bandits know the Bandit roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake becomes Raider and steals the Minnow Treats. Bandits may lie about time, Treat State, and role claims at Morning Business. Dooker contributes 3 distinct d12s and this anchor: Chronological first wake: ordinary observation. Second wake: one Glimpse, learning Bandit-aligned or Not Bandit-aligned for one chosen player at that moment. Third wake: one Trip, view one Hammock card and optionally exchange it with any player's current card without viewing the card taken from that player.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

A strong Bandit claim uses mostly true timing information and changes only the detail needed to protect the Raider. Dooker can withhold which wake contained the Glimpse or Trip, creating uncertainty without fabricating extra information. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Do not let every Bandit receive additional special powers. The hostile team's strength comes from coordination, uncertainty, and conversions. Three wakes are strong. Limit to exactly one Glimpse and one Trip, with no full exact-role scan. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 02: BANDIT x ITCHY
Primary lens: card-state ambiguity

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Bandit and Itchy can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Bandit contributes 1 d12 and the following mechanical anchor: Starting Bandits know the Bandit roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake becomes Raider and steals the Minnow Treats. Bandits may lie about time, Treat State, and role claims at Morning Business. Itchy contributes normally 1 d12; on an activated observation night, 2 distinct d12s and this anchor: When activated, the app gives a deliberately incomplete clue after the raid: two player identities are shown, exactly one of whom is the Raider when the rules can generate a valid decoy. Itchy is never told which one.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

A strong Bandit claim uses mostly true timing information and changes only the detail needed to protect the Raider. Because everyone knows Itchy may fail to notice anything, a claimed no-clue night is plausible but not self-proving. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Do not let every Bandit receive additional special powers. The hostile team's strength comes from coordination, uncertainty, and conversions. The clue activates only one third of nights by default and must remain partial. Never reveal an exact Bandit identity. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 03: BANDIT x TROUBLE

INTERACTION 04: BANDIT x BUSINESS

A strong Bandit claim uses mostly true timing information and changes only the detail needed to protect the Raider. INTERACTION 05: BANDIT x SNUGGLER
Primary lens: information asymmetry

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Bandit and Snuggler can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Bandit contributes 1 d12 and the following mechanical anchor: Starting Bandits know the Bandit roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake becomes Raider and steals the Minnow Treats. Bandits may lie about time, Treat State, and role claims at Morning Business. Snuggler contributes 1 d12 and this anchor: Before Hour 1, choose two players to become Snuggle-Bonded. Those players privately learn each other's identity as bonded partners. If either bonded player is caught/revealed by the final Paw Point resolution, the other bonded player is also revealed as part of the same resolution.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

A strong Bandit claim uses mostly true timing information and changes only the detail needed to protect the Raider. Odd cooperation may come from a real bond, a Bandit alliance, Guardian trust, or ordinary social play. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Do not let every Bandit receive additional special powers. The hostile team's strength comes from coordination, uncertainty, and conversions. Bond follows the players, not the cards. Card swaps do not erase or move the bond. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 06: BANDIT x HUNTER
Primary lens: bluff compatibility

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Bandit and Hunter can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Bandit contributes 1 d12 and the following mechanical anchor: Starting Bandits know the Bandit roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake becomes Raider and steals the Minnow Treats. Bandits may lie about time, Treat State, and role claims at Morning Business. Hunter contributes 2 distinct d12s and this anchor: Hunter and Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. After Hunter's later chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter secretly places one Hunt Mark on a player. If Hunter is caught by the final vote while still loyal, the marked player is checked: if that player is Bandit-aligned they are also caught; if not, nothing happens. Converted Hunter loses the Hunt Mark effect.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

A strong Bandit claim uses mostly true timing information and changes only the detail needed to protect the Raider. A converted Hunter can continue behaving like Guardian's trusted partner because the original trust was genuine. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Do not let every Bandit receive additional special powers. The hostile team's strength comes from coordination, uncertainty, and conversions. Conversion must stay rare. Default low d6 threshold is 1-2, not 1-3. The Hunt Mark only fires if Hunter is caught and only harms a Bandit-aligned target. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 07: BANDIT x GUARDIAN
Primary lens: conversion timing

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Bandit and Guardian can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Bandit contributes 1 d12 and the following mechanical anchor: Starting Bandits know the Bandit roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake becomes Raider and steals the Minnow Treats. Bandits may lie about time, Treat State, and role claims at Morning Business. Guardian contributes 1 d12 and this anchor: Guardian and Hunter recognize each other before Hour 1. Once per game, immediately before Paw Point locks, Guardian privately protects one other player. A protected player cannot be the sole player caught by the vote. Guardian is never told whether Hunter converted.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

A strong Bandit claim uses mostly true timing information and changes only the detail needed to protect the Raider. Guardian can claim trust in Hunter without revealing whether they intend to spend protection there. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Do not let every Bandit receive additional special powers. The hostile team's strength comes from coordination, uncertainty, and conversions. Guardian cannot protect self. Protection does not prevent card swaps, Snuggle bonds, role clues, or Hunter conversion. It only changes final vote capture. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 08: DOOKER x ITCHY
Primary lens: treat-state evidence

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Dooker and Itchy can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Dooker contributes 3 distinct d12s and the following mechanical anchor: Chronological first wake: ordinary observation. Second wake: one Glimpse, learning Bandit-aligned or Not Bandit-aligned for one chosen player at that moment. Third wake: one Trip, view one Hammock card and optionally exchange it with any player's current card without viewing the card taken from that player. Itchy contributes normally 1 d12; on an activated observation night, 2 distinct d12s and this anchor: When activated, the app gives a deliberately incomplete clue after the raid: two player identities are shown, exactly one of whom is the Raider when the rules can generate a valid decoy. Itchy is never told which one.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

Dooker can withhold which wake contained the Glimpse or Trip, creating uncertainty without fabricating extra information. Because everyone knows Itchy may fail to notice anything, a claimed no-clue night is plausible but not self-proving. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Three wakes are strong. Limit to exactly one Glimpse and one Trip, with no full exact-role scan. The clue activates only one third of nights by default and must remain partial. Never reveal an exact Bandit identity. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 09: DOOKER x TROUBLE
Primary lens: wake-hour collision

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Dooker and Trouble can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Dooker contributes 3 distinct d12s and the following mechanical anchor: Chronological first wake: ordinary observation. Second wake: one Glimpse, learning Bandit-aligned or Not Bandit-aligned for one chosen player at that moment. Third wake: one Trip, view one Hammock card and optionally exchange it with any player's current card without viewing the card taken from that player. Trouble contributes 1 d12 and this anchor: At Trouble's wake, blind-swap the current cards of two other players without seeing either card. If Trouble is awake in the same hour that the Raider steals the Minnow Treats, Trouble becomes In On It before resolving the swap.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

Dooker can withhold which wake contained the Glimpse or Trip, creating uncertainty without fabricating extra information. A converted Trouble can truthfully report the swap while selectively lying about who was awake or whether the treats were present. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Three wakes are strong. Limit to exactly one Glimpse and one Trip, with no full exact-role scan. Catching Trouble alone does not count as catching the Raider. Only one Trouble card exists. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

STARTING QUESTION

Snuggler contributes 1 d12 and this anchor: Before Hour 1, choose two players to become Snuggle-Bonded. Those players privately learn each other's identity as bonded partners. If either bonded player is caught/revealed by the final Paw Point resolution, the other bonded player is also revealed as part of the same resolution.

If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random.
