Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

Because everyone knows Itchy may fail to notice anything, a claimed no-clue night is plausible but not self-proving. Odd cooperation may come from a real bond, a Bandit alliance, Guardian trust, or ordinary social play. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

The clue activates only one third of nights by default and must remain partial. Never reveal an exact Bandit identity. Bond follows the players, not the cards. Card swaps do not erase or move the bond. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 17: ITCHY x HUNTER
Primary lens: wake-hour collision

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Itchy and Hunter can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Itchy contributes normally 1 d12; on an activated observation night, 2 distinct d12s and the following mechanical anchor: When activated, the app gives a deliberately incomplete clue after the raid: two player identities are shown, exactly one of whom is the Raider when the rules can generate a valid decoy. Itchy is never told which one. Hunter contributes 2 distinct d12s and this anchor: Hunter and Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. After Hunter's later chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter secretly places one Hunt Mark on a player. If Hunter is caught by the final vote while still loyal, the marked player is checked: if that player is Bandit-aligned they are also caught; if not, nothing happens. Converted Hunter loses the Hunt Mark effect.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

Because everyone knows Itchy may fail to notice anything, a claimed no-clue night is plausible but not self-proving. A converted Hunter can continue behaving like Guardian's trusted partner because the original trust was genuine. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

The clue activates only one third of nights by default and must remain partial. Never reveal an exact Bandit identity. Conversion must stay rare. Default low d6 threshold is 1-2, not 1-3. The Hunt Mark only fires if Hunter is caught and only harms a Bandit-aligned target. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 18: ITCHY x GUARDIAN
Primary lens: card-state ambiguity

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Itchy and Guardian can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Itchy contributes normally 1 d12; on an activated observation night, 2 distinct d12s and the following mechanical anchor: When activated, the app gives a deliberately incomplete clue after the raid: two player identities are shown, exactly one of whom is the Raider when the rules can generate a valid decoy. Itchy is never told which one. Guardian contributes 1 d12 and this anchor: Guardian and Hunter recognize each other before Hour 1. Once per game, immediately before Paw Point locks, Guardian privately protects one other player. A protected player cannot be the sole player caught by the vote. Guardian is never told whether Hunter converted.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

Because everyone knows Itchy may fail to notice anything, a claimed no-clue night is plausible but not self-proving. Guardian can claim trust in Hunter without revealing whether they intend to spend protection there. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

The clue activates only one third of nights by default and must remain partial. Never reveal an exact Bandit identity. Guardian cannot protect self. Protection does not prevent card swaps, Snuggle bonds, role clues, or Hunter conversion. It only changes final vote capture. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 19: TROUBLE x BUSINESS
Primary lens: trust and betrayal

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Trouble and Business can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Trouble contributes 1 d12 and the following mechanical anchor: At Trouble's wake, blind-swap the current cards of two other players without seeing either card. If Trouble is awake in the same hour that the Raider steals the Minnow Treats, Trouble becomes In On It before resolving the swap. Business contributes 1 d12 and this anchor: No unique special action. Business sees Treat State and co-wakers. If Business wakes alone, it may privately inspect one other player's wake hour for that round.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

A converted Trouble can truthfully report the swap while selectively lying about who was awake or whether the treats were present. Business claims are simple enough for Bandits to imitate, which is important for social deduction. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Catching Trouble alone does not count as catching the Raider. Only one Trouble card exists. There are fourteen Business cards in the twenty-four-card pool so ordinary evidence remains common. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

Snuggler contributes 1 d12 and the following mechanical anchor: Before Hour 1, choose two players to become Snuggle-Bonded. Those players privately learn each other's identity as bonded partners. If either bonded player is caught/revealed by the final Paw Point resolution, the other bonded player is also revealed as part of the same resolution. Guardian contributes 1 d12 and this anchor: Guardian and Hunter recognize each other before Hour 1. Once per game, immediately before Paw Point locks, Guardian privately protects one other player. A protected player cannot be the sole player caught by the vote. Guardian is never told whether Hunter converted.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

Odd cooperation may come from a real bond, a Bandit alliance, Guardian trust, or ordinary social play. Guardian can claim trust in Hunter without revealing whether they intend to spend protection there. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Bond follows the players, not the cards. Card swaps do not erase or move the bond. Guardian cannot protect self. Protection does not prevent card swaps, Snuggle bonds, role clues, or Hunter conversion. It only changes final vote capture. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

INTERACTION 28: HUNTER x GUARDIAN
Primary lens: vote-resolution pressure

STARTING QUESTION

Test whether Hunter and Guardian can coexist without either role automatically proving the other player's allegiance. Hunter contributes 2 distinct d12s and the following mechanical anchor: Hunter and Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. After Hunter's later chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter secretly places one Hunt Mark on a player. If Hunter is caught by the final vote while still loyal, the marked player is checked: if that player is Bandit-aligned they are also caught; if not, nothing happens. Converted Hunter loses the Hunt Mark effect. Guardian contributes 1 d12 and this anchor: Guardian and Hunter recognize each other before Hour 1. Once per game, immediately before Paw Point locks, Guardian privately protects one other player. A protected player cannot be the sole player caught by the vote. Guardian is never told whether Hunter converted.

TIMING TEST

Run the pair in at least three clock configurations. First, give their wake hours no overlap. Second, force one overlap. Third, place one action immediately before or after the Raider theft. Record what each player can know as a fact at each step. A healthy interaction creates a story that becomes clearer only when several facts are combined. It should not create a single UI message that says, in effect, "the other player is guilty."

CARD-MOVEMENT TEST

If either role can be affected by Dooker or Trouble elsewhere in the lobby, repeat the scenario after one card movement. Starting-role actions must still resolve from the starting role. Final-card and status fields must remain separate. The replay should be able to explain the sequence in plain language without requiring players to understand internal event IDs.

CLAIM TEST

A converted Hunter can continue behaving like Guardian's trusted partner because the original trust was genuine. Guardian can claim trust in Hunter without revealing whether they intend to spend protection there. Ask playtesters to give both a truthful claim and a plausible false claim for each role. If one role cannot be imitated by a Bandit at all, reduce the precision or public verifiability of its information. If every possible claim is equally unverifiable, strengthen the shared wake evidence rather than adding another hard scan.

BALANCE TEST

Conversion must stay rare. Default low d6 threshold is 1-2, not 1-3. The Hunt Mark only fires if Hunter is caught and only harms a Bandit-aligned target. Guardian cannot protect self. Protection does not prevent card swaps, Snuggle bonds, role clues, or Hunter conversion. It only changes final vote capture. Watch for stacking. Two individually fair abilities may become unfair when one verifies the other. Conversely, two uncertainty roles may create so much noise that the vote becomes random. The target experience is constrained ambiguity: several stories remain possible at the start of Morning Business, but careful use of wake times and Treat State can eliminate some of them.

UI / PRIVACY TEST

Confirm that each player sees only their own raw dice, role prompts, and entitled facts. The mere presence of an action button must not reveal that the paired role exists. Reconnection must rebuild the same private state. Spectator and host screens must not expose raw private d6 or d12 values.

PASS CRITERIA

This pair passes when players can explain at least two plausible narratives before discussion, at least one objective fact can eventually distinguish those narratives, no role learns more than its text promises, and the post-game Trail of Trouble can reconstruct the exact order of events.

PART XI - SCENARIO LABORATORY

The following scenarios are intentionally repetitive in structure but different in state. They are QA fixtures, not narrative content. Every build should be able to reproduce them from a deterministic test seed.

SCENARIO 001: EARLY RAID

PURPOSE
Raider wakes in Hours 1-3 before most players.

SETUP
Use a deterministic seed and explicitly record every Starting Card, Hammock card, d12 result, d6 result, status flag, and target choice. Force the scenario condition rather than waiting for random generation. Keep at least one unrelated Business player in the fixture whenever player count allows so the test still contains ordinary evidence.

EXPECTED NIGHT BEHAVIOR
The Burrow Clock advances in numeric order. Clients wake only at their assigned hours. Treat State changes only when the Raider theft resolves. Starting-role actions resolve even if that player's current card was moved earlier, unless the specific role text says otherwise. Trouble and Hunter conversions are player-bound status changes. Snuggle Bond and Guardian/Hunter recognition are also player-bound.

EXPECTED INFORMATION BOUNDARIES
Raw d6 and d12 values remain private. A player may infer another wake hour from seeing them awake, from a legal Business solo inspection, or from a claim, but the UI must not leak the number merely because it exists in authoritative state. Dooker receives only one Glimpse result and one viewed Hammock card. Itchy receives only the clue allowed by its activation. Guardian never sees Hunter's conversion flag.

MORNING-BUSINESS QUESTIONS
Ask testers:
- What facts do you personally know?
- Which statements are only claims?
- What is the earliest possible theft hour?
- What is the latest possible theft hour?
- Which card movements are certain?
- Could Hunter or Trouble have changed allegiance?
- Does catching the current suspect actually catch the Raider?

RESOLUTION CHECK
Apply Guardian protection first. Determine the top eligible Paw Point target or all tied eligible targets. Apply Snuggle Bond. Apply loyal Hunter's conditional Hunt Mark if Hunter was caught. Then check whether the historical Raider is in the final caught set. Display the primary result and every secondary role result separately.

PASS CRITERIA
The scenario passes only if the final result can be explained in plain language, every client received exactly its authorized information, no stale pre-swap role label controls the wrong win condition, the Raider tag remains attached to the actual thief, and the replay reproduces the same state from the event log.

SCENARIO 013: ITCHY FAILS TO NOTICE

PURPOSE
Itchy d6 is 1-4 and gets no special clue.

SETUP
Use a deterministic seed and explicitly record every Starting Card, Hammock card, d12 result, d6 result, status flag, and target choice. Force the scenario condition rather than waiting for random generation. Keep at least one unrelated Business player in the fixture whenever player count allows so the test still contains ordinary evidence.

EXPECTED NIGHT BEHAVIOR
The Burrow Clock advances in numeric order. Clients wake only at their assigned hours. Treat State changes only when the Raider theft resolves. Starting-role actions resolve even if that player's current card was moved earlier, unless the specific role text says otherwise. Trouble and Hunter conversions are player-bound status changes. Snuggle Bond and Guardian/Hunter recognition are also player-bound.

EXPECTED INFORMATION BOUNDARIES
Raw d6 and d12 values remain private. A player may infer another wake hour from seeing them awake, from a legal Business solo inspection, or from a claim, but the UI must not leak the number merely because it exists in authoritative state. Dooker receives only one Glimpse result and one viewed Hammock card. Itchy receives only the clue allowed by its activation. Guardian never sees Hunter's conversion flag.

MORNING-BUSINESS QUESTIONS
Ask testers:
- What facts do you personally know?
- Which statements are only claims?
- What is the earliest possible theft hour?
- What is the latest possible theft hour?
- Which card movements are certain?
- Could Hunter or Trouble have changed allegiance?
- Does catching the current suspect actually catch the Raider?

RESOLUTION CHECK
Apply Guardian protection first. Determine the top eligible Paw Point target or all tied eligible targets. Apply Snuggle Bond. Apply loyal Hunter's conditional Hunt Mark if Hunter was caught. Then check whether the historical Raider is in the final caught set. Display the primary result and every secondary role result separately.

PASS CRITERIA
The scenario passes only if the final result can be explained in plain language, every client received exactly its authorized information, no stale pre-swap role label controls the wrong win condition, the Raider tag remains attached to the actual thief, and the replay reproduces the same state from the event log.

SCENARIO 028: TOP VOTE TIE

PURPOSE
Two unprotected players tie for highest vote.

SETUP
Use a deterministic seed and explicitly record every Starting Card, Hammock card, d12 result, d6 result, status flag, and target choice. Force the scenario condition rather than waiting for random generation. Keep at least one unrelated Business player in the fixture whenever player count allows so the test still contains ordinary evidence.

EXPECTED NIGHT BEHAVIOR
The Burrow Clock advances in numeric order. Clients wake only at their assigned hours. Treat State changes only when the Raider theft resolves. Starting-role actions resolve even if that player's current card was moved earlier, unless the specific role text says otherwise. Trouble and Hunter conversions are player-bound status changes. Snuggle Bond and Guardian/Hunter recognition are also player-bound.

EXPECTED INFORMATION BOUNDARIES
Raw d6 and d12 values remain private. A player may infer another wake hour from seeing them awake, from a legal Business solo inspection, or from a claim, but the UI must not leak the number merely because it exists in authoritative state. Dooker receives only one Glimpse result and one viewed Hammock card. Itchy receives only the clue allowed by its activation. Guardian never sees Hunter's conversion flag.

Then check whether the historical Raider is in the final caught set. Display the primary result and every secondary role result separately.

PASS CRITERIA
The scenario passes only if the final result can be explained in plain language, every client received exactly its authorized information, no stale pre-swap role label controls the wrong win condition, the Raider tag remains attached to the actual thief, and the replay reproduces the same state from the event log.

SCENARIO 058: MULTIPLE BANDITS SAME WINDOW

PURPOSE
Several Bandits are awake when Raider theft occurs.

SETUP
Use a deterministic seed and explicitly record every Starting Card, Hammock card, d12 result, d6 result, status flag, and target choice. Force the scenario condition rather than waiting for random generation. Keep at least one unrelated Business player in the fixture whenever player count allows so the test still contains ordinary evidence.

EXPECTED NIGHT BEHAVIOR
The Burrow Clock advances in numeric order. Clients wake only at their assigned hours. Treat State changes only when the Raider theft resolves. Starting-role actions resolve even if that player's current card was moved earlier, unless the specific role text says otherwise. Trouble and Hunter conversions are player-bound status changes. Snuggle Bond and Guardian/Hunter recognition are also player-bound.

EXPECTED INFORMATION BOUNDARIES
Raw d6 and d12 values remain private. A player may infer another wake hour from seeing them awake, from a legal Business solo inspection, or from a claim, but the UI must not leak the number merely because it exists in authoritative state. Dooker receives only one Glimpse result and one viewed Hammock card. Itchy receives only the clue allowed by its activation. Guardian never sees Hunter's conversion flag.

PART XII - IMPLEMENTATION SPECIFICATION

31. AUTHORITATIVE STATE MACHINE

LOBBY
-> ACTIVE_SET_BUILD
-> DEAL
-> ROLE_REVEAL
-> PRE_NIGHT_RELATIONSHIPS
-> WAKE_ROLLS
-> ITCHY_D6
-> RAIDER_SELECTION
-> HOUR_1
-> HOUR_2
-> HOUR_3
-> HOUR_4
-> HOUR_5
-> HOUR_6
-> HOUR_7
-> HOUR_8
-> HOUR_9
-> HOUR_10
-> HOUR_11
-> HOUR_12
-> MORNING_BUSINESS
-> GUARDIAN_PROTECTION
-> VOTE_LOCK
-> VOTE_RESOLUTION
-> SECONDARY_RESOLUTION
-> RESULT
-> TRAIL_OF_TROUBLE
-> REMATCH

Every transition must be idempotent. Reconnecting clients request their authorized current state rather than replaying secret history they were never entitled to see.

32. EVENT LOG

Suggested event IDs:

MATCH_CREATED
ACTIVE_SET_LOCKED
CARD_DEALT
HAMMOCK_CARD_PLACED
STARTING_ROLE_REVEALED
SNUGGLE_BOND_CREATED
GUARDIAN_HUNTER_RECOGNITION
DIE_SELECTED
D6_ROLLED
D12_ROLLED
DUPLICATE_WAKE_REROLL_REQUIRED
WAKE_SCHEDULE_LOCKED

RAIDER_SELECTED
HOUR_OPENED
PLAYER_WOKE
TREAT_STATE_OBSERVED
RAID_COMPLETED
TROUBLE_CONVERTED
HUNTER_OVERLAP_TRIGGERED
HUNTER_CONVERSION_ROLL
HUNTER_CONVERTED

DOOKER_TRIP

GUARDIAN_PROTECTION_SET
VOTE_CAST
VOTE_LOCKED

BOND_EXTRA_REVEAL
HUNT_MARK_CHECKED
RAIDER_CAUGHT
RAIDER_ESCAPED
RESULT_COMPUTED
REPLAY_OPENED

Each event carries a visibility policy:
PUBLIC
PLAYER_ONLY(player_id)
ROLE_GROUP_ONLY(group_id)
POSTGAME_ONLY
SERVER_ONLY

33. ROLE DATA MODEL

A role definition should include:

id
display_name
visual_variety
starting_team
wake_roll_count
unique_wake_required
d6_rule
pre_night_action
hour_action
conversion_hooks
vote_hook
resolution_hook
claim_help
accessibility_text
icon_id
card_art_id

Player state should include:

player_id
starting_role_id
current_card_id
current_team
raider_boolean
trouble_accomplice_boolean
hunter_converted_boolean
wake_hours[]
itchy_activation
snuggle_partner_ids[]
guardian_partner_id
guardian_protection_target
hunt_mark_target
observed_treat_states{}
observed_cowakers{}
verified_private_facts[]
player_notes[]

34. DICE SERVICE

The dice service must generate server-authoritative cryptographically adequate random results for fairness in online play.

A joystick flick is the user gesture that commits the roll request.

The server returns the result only to the rolling player's authorized channel plus the hidden match state.

The visual die animation is presentation. It must land on the server-approved result.

For local/offline play, the device may generate the result locally, but the same interface should be used so the game logic does not diverge.

Duplicate d12 handling for multi-wake roles must be deterministic:
- accept the first result;
- if a later result duplicates an already accepted hour for that same player, prompt another d12 roll;
- do not reroll the previously accepted hour;
- do not tell other players a reroll occurred.

35. CARD-MOVEMENT ENGINE

Card movement is position-based.

Trouble swaps PLAYER_A.current_card with PLAYER_B.current_card.

Dooker Trip swaps one selected HAMMOCK_SLOT.current_card with PLAYER.current_card.

The system never mutates Starting Role.

After every card movement, recompute card-derived current team, then reapply player-bound status overrides in this order:
1. Raider remains Bandit-side.
2. Trouble accomplice remains Bandit-side.
3. Hunter conversion remains Bandit-side.
4. Any future explicit status override.

This order guarantees that a Raider cannot accidentally become Business-side merely because Dooker moved the Bandit card after the theft.

36. RESOLUTION ENGINE

Vote resolution algorithm:

1. Count one Paw Point per active voter.
2. Remove Guardian-protected target from eligibility for sole/top capture as specified by the protection rule.
3. Find highest vote total among eligible targets.
4. Catch every eligible target tied at that highest total.
5. If no target has a legal capture total under the chosen minimum-vote rule, catch nobody.
6. Apply Snuggle Bond extra catches.
7. If loyal Hunter is caught, test Hunt Mark and add marked target only when Bandit-aligned.
8. Determine whether historical Raider is now caught.
9. Set primary result.
10. Generate secondary summaries.

The engine must resolve from stored state, not from animation callbacks.

PART XIII - ACCESSIBILITY AND SAFETY

37. ACCESSIBLE PRIVATE INFORMATION

Every private fact must exist in text form.

A blind or low-vision player must be able to:
- identify selected die;
- roll by joystick flick or accessible equivalent;
- hear/read the die result privately;
- know required wake hours;
- know when their hour is active;
- identify co-wakers by accessible player name;
- know Treat State;
- choose legal role-action targets;
- participate in voting.

Itchy's partial observation is delivered as names/text, not as a purely visual silhouette puzzle.

Card art is decorative support, not the only carrier of role rules.

38. REDUCED MOTION

Reduced-motion mode replaces tumbling dice with a short controlled roll/flip sequence while preserving the joystick-flick action.

Wake transitions fade rather than zoom.

Ferret celebration animations use short pose changes instead of rapid war-dance loops.

No information depends on animation speed.

39. FERRET-WELFARE LANGUAGE

The game is not husbandry advice.

Minnow Treats are fictionalized and do not specify feeding quantity.

Itchy's scratching is not presented as normal because of Cinnamon coloration.

No role's morality or competence is said to come from coat color.

Ferrets are shown as mischievous and curious across all appearances.

The codex may explain that persistent itching, major behavior changes, or health concerns in a real ferret should be discussed with an appropriate veterinarian rather than interpreted through the game.

PART XIV - SYSTEM QA CHECKLIST

40. DICE QA

For d6:
- /1/ always selects d6.
- /2/ must deselect d6 and select d12.
- joystick navigation without a deliberate flick does not roll.
- result is private.
- Hunter low-roll conversion threshold is exactly 1-2 in base rules.
- Itchy activation threshold is exactly 5-6 in base rules.
- Bandit Raider tiebreak uses lowest d6 and rerolls only tied lowest candidates.

For d12:
- /2/ always selects d12.
- values 1-12 are equiprobable unless an explicitly named accessibility/testing mode overrides randomness.
- multi-wake duplicates reroll.
- raw values are private.
- wake order sorts ascending after all rolls finish.

41. ROLE QA

Bandit:
- earliest starting-Bandit hour produces Raider.
- Raider steals exactly once.
- card movement cannot erase Raider tag.

Dooker:
- exactly three unique hours.
- one Glimpse maximum.
- one Trip maximum.
- Trip views Hammock card but not displaced player card.

Itchy:
- d6 1-4 no special clue.
- d6 5-6 extra wake and partial clue.
- no exact Raider reveal.

Trouble:
- cannot swap self.
- must swap two distinct other players.
- conversion occurs on theft-hour overlap before swap.
- caught accomplice alone does not satisfy Business win.

Business:
- one d12.
- solo wake permits one wake-hour inspection.
- shared wake permits no solo inspection.

Snuggler:
- bond follows players.
- bond survives card swaps.
- bond extra reveal uses final caught set.

Hunter:
- two unique d12s.
- first Bandit overlap triggers one d6 only.
- Guardian never sees result.
- low roll converts.
- converted Hunter has no Hunt Mark effect.

Guardian:
- one d12.
- knows starting Hunter.
- cannot protect self.
- protection can shield converted Hunter without warning.

42. PRIVACY QA

The following are forbidden leaks during the round:
- another player's exact d6;
- another player's exact d12 unless legally inspected;
- whether Itchy activated;
- whether Hunter converted;
- which Hammock card Dooker viewed;
- whether Guardian selected Hunter until resolution;
- another player's starting card unless a role reveals it;
- hidden current-card movement before a player is entitled to know it.

The host application may need all data to resolve the match but must not render hidden values on a shared display.

PART XV - EXTENDED PLAYTEST PROBES

These probes are designed for repeated human sessions. They are intentionally detailed so the dossier can function as a long-term balance notebook rather than only a pitch document.

PLAYTEST PROBE 001: BANDIT BLUFFING AFTER AN EARLY THEFT

HYPOTHESIS

The game should remain understandable and socially interesting when testing bandit bluffing after an early theft. Players should be able to describe the important state in ordinary language without reading the implementation log, while still having enough uncertainty to debate what happened.

METHOD

Run at least five rounds that intentionally increase the frequency of this condition. Use fixed test seeds for the first two rounds so designers can compare groups against the same evidence graph. Use true random dice for the remaining rounds. Record player count, Active Set, starting roles, Hammock cards, all private d12 values, all d6 values, every card movement, conversion status, bond, protection, Hunt Mark, claims made during Morning Business, vote totals, and final result.

PLAYER INTERVIEW

Before showing Trail of Trouble, ask each player:
1. Who do you think was the Raider?
2. What single fact mattered most?
3. What statement did you distrust most?
4. Did you understand your own role action?
5. Did any UI element reveal information you felt you should not know?
6. Did you feel your role had a meaningful decision?
7. Could you explain why the final result happened?

DESIGNER OBSERVATION

Watch for automatic certainty before Morning Business reaches its midpoint. Also watch for the opposite failure, where no claim can be tested against any objective timing evidence. A good round has at least two coherent narratives early in discussion and progressively narrows them through wake-hour facts, Treat State, legal card movement, and relationship information.

BALANCE FLAGS

Mark TOO STRONG if one role repeatedly proves the Raider without meaningful counterplay.
Mark TOO WEAK if the role's information never affects discussion or voting.
Mark TOO CHAOTIC if players stop reasoning about the timeline and simply guess.
Mark TOO SOLVED if the same optimal claim script works every round.
Mark PRIVACY FAIL if any client or shared screen exposes raw dice or hidden status incorrectly.

ACCEPTANCE TARGET

The probe passes when at least seventy percent of experienced groups can reconstruct the correct hidden sequence after the reveal, while pre-reveal accusation accuracy remains meaningfully below certainty. New-player groups should at minimum understand which parts of their own story were facts and which were deductions.

PLAYTEST PROBE 012: BUSINESS SOLO-PEEK SELECTING A HIGH-VALUE MULTIWAKE ROLE

HYPOTHESIS

The game should remain understandable and socially interesting when testing business solo-peek selecting a high-value multiwake role. Players should be able to describe the important state in ordinary language without reading the implementation log, while still having enough uncertainty to debate what happened.

METHOD

Run at least five rounds that intentionally increase the frequency of this condition. Use fixed test seeds for the first two rounds so designers can compare groups against the same evidence graph. Use true random dice for the remaining rounds. Record player count, Active Set, starting roles, Hammock cards, all private d12 values, all d6 values, every card movement, conversion status, bond, protection, Hunt Mark, claims made during Morning Business, vote totals, and final result.

PLAYER INTERVIEW

Before showing Trail of Trouble, ask each player:
1. Who do you think was the Raider?
2. What single fact mattered most?
3. What statement did you distrust most?
4. Did you understand your own role action?
5. Did any UI element reveal information you felt you should not know?
6. Did you feel your role had a meaningful decision?
7. Could you explain why the final result happened?

DESIGNER OBSERVATION

Watch for automatic certainty before Morning Business reaches its midpoint. Also watch for the opposite failure, where no claim can be tested against any objective timing evidence. A good round has at least two coherent narratives early in discussion and progressively narrows them through wake-hour facts, Treat State, legal card movement, and relationship information.

BALANCE FLAGS

Mark TOO STRONG if one role repeatedly proves the Raider without meaningful counterplay.
Mark TOO WEAK if the role's information never affects discussion or voting.
Mark TOO CHAOTIC if players stop reasoning about the timeline and simply guess.
Mark TOO SOLVED if the same optimal claim script works every round.
Mark PRIVACY FAIL if any client or shared screen exposes raw dice or hidden status incorrectly.

ACCEPTANCE TARGET

The probe passes when at least seventy percent of experienced groups can reconstruct the correct hidden sequence after the reveal, while pre-reveal accusation accuracy remains meaningfully below certainty. New-player groups should at minimum understand which parts of their own story were facts and which were deductions.

PLAYTEST PROBE 027: THREE-CARD HAMMOCK INFORMATION BECOMING TOO SOLVABLE

HYPOTHESIS

The game should remain understandable and socially interesting when testing three-card hammock information becoming too solvable. Players should be able to describe the important state in ordinary language without reading the implementation log, while still having enough uncertainty to debate what happened.

METHOD

Run at least five rounds that intentionally increase the frequency of this condition. Use fixed test seeds for the first two rounds so designers can compare groups against the same evidence graph. Use true random dice for the remaining rounds. Record player count, Active Set, starting roles, Hammock cards, all private d12 values, all d6 values, every card movement, conversion status, bond, protection, Hunt Mark, claims made during Morning Business, vote totals, and final result.

PLAYER INTERVIEW

Before showing Trail of Trouble, ask each player:
1. Who do you think was the Raider?
2. What single fact mattered most?
3. What statement did you distrust most?
4. Did you understand your own role action?
5. Did any UI element reveal information you felt you should not know?
6. Did you feel your role had a meaningful decision?
7. Could you explain why the final result happened?

DESIGNER OBSERVATION

Watch for automatic certainty before Morning Business reaches its midpoint. Also watch for the opposite failure, where no claim can be tested against any objective timing evidence. A good round has at least two coherent narratives early in discussion and progressively narrows them through wake-hour facts, Treat State, legal card movement, and relationship information.

BALANCE FLAGS

Mark TOO STRONG if one role repeatedly proves the Raider without meaningful counterplay.
Mark TOO WEAK if the role's information never affects discussion or voting.
Mark TOO CHAOTIC if players stop reasoning about the timeline and simply guess.
Mark TOO SOLVED if the same optimal claim script works every round.
Mark PRIVACY FAIL if any client or shared screen exposes raw dice or hidden status incorrectly.

ACCEPTANCE TARGET

The probe passes when at least seventy percent of experienced groups can reconstruct the correct hidden sequence after the reveal, while pre-reveal accusation accuracy remains meaningfully below certainty. New-player groups should at minimum understand which parts of their own story were facts and which were deductions.

The probe passes when at least seventy percent of experienced groups can reconstruct the correct hidden sequence after the reveal, while pre-reveal accusation accuracy remains meaningfully below certainty. PLAYTEST PROBE 042: GUARDIAN PLAYER-HELD WHILE HUNTER IS IN HAMMOCK

HYPOTHESIS

The game should remain understandable and socially interesting when testing guardian player-held while hunter is in hammock. Players should be able to describe the important state in ordinary language without reading the implementation log, while still having enough uncertainty to debate what happened.

METHOD

Run at least five rounds that intentionally increase the frequency of this condition. Use fixed test seeds for the first two rounds so designers can compare groups against the same evidence graph. Use true random dice for the remaining rounds. Record player count, Active Set, starting roles, Hammock cards, all private d12 values, all d6 values, every card movement, conversion status, bond, protection, Hunt Mark, claims made during Morning Business, vote totals, and final result.

PLAYER INTERVIEW

Before showing Trail of Trouble, ask each player:
1. Who do you think was the Raider?
2. What single fact mattered most?
3. What statement did you distrust most?
4. Did you understand your own role action?
5. Did any UI element reveal information you felt you should not know?
6. Did you feel your role had a meaningful decision?
7. Could you explain why the final result happened?

DESIGNER OBSERVATION

Watch for automatic certainty before Morning Business reaches its midpoint. Also watch for the opposite failure, where no claim can be tested against any objective timing evidence. A good round has at least two coherent narratives early in discussion and progressively narrows them through wake-hour facts, Treat State, legal card movement, and relationship information.

BALANCE FLAGS

Mark TOO STRONG if one role repeatedly proves the Raider without meaningful counterplay.
Mark TOO WEAK if the role's information never affects discussion or voting.
Mark TOO CHAOTIC if players stop reasoning about the timeline and simply guess.
Mark TOO SOLVED if the same optimal claim script works every round.
Mark PRIVACY FAIL if any client or shared screen exposes raw dice or hidden status incorrectly.

ACCEPTANCE TARGET

The probe passes when at least seventy percent of experienced groups can reconstruct the correct hidden sequence after the reveal, while pre-reveal accusation accuracy remains meaningfully below certainty. New-player groups should at minimum understand which parts of their own story were facts and which were deductions.

New-player groups should at minimum understand which parts of their own story were facts and which were deductions.

PLAYTEST PROBE 057: RECONNECT AFTER HUNTER CONVERSION

HYPOTHESIS

The game should remain understandable and socially interesting when testing reconnect after hunter conversion. Players should be able to describe the important state in ordinary language without reading the implementation log, while still having enough uncertainty to debate what happened.

METHOD

Run at least five rounds that intentionally increase the frequency of this condition. Use fixed test seeds for the first two rounds so designers can compare groups against the same evidence graph. Use true random dice for the remaining rounds. Record player count, Active Set, starting roles, Hammock cards, all private d12 values, all d6 values, every card movement, conversion status, bond, protection, Hunt Mark, claims made during Morning Business, vote totals, and final result.

PLAYER INTERVIEW

Before showing Trail of Trouble, ask each player:
1. Who do you think was the Raider?
2. What single fact mattered most?
3. What statement did you distrust most?
4. Did you understand your own role action?
5. Did any UI element reveal information you felt you should not know?
6. Did you feel your role had a meaningful decision?
7. Could you explain why the final result happened?

DESIGNER OBSERVATION

Watch for automatic certainty before Morning Business reaches its midpoint. Also watch for the opposite failure, where no claim can be tested against any objective timing evidence. A good round has at least two coherent narratives early in discussion and progressively narrows them through wake-hour facts, Treat State, legal card movement, and relationship information.

BALANCE FLAGS

Mark TOO STRONG if one role repeatedly proves the Raider without meaningful counterplay.
Mark TOO WEAK if the role's information never affects discussion or voting.
Mark TOO CHAOTIC if players stop reasoning about the timeline and simply guess.
Mark TOO SOLVED if the same optimal claim script works every round.
Mark PRIVACY FAIL if any client or shared screen exposes raw dice or hidden status incorrectly.

ACCEPTANCE TARGET

The probe passes when at least seventy percent of experienced groups can reconstruct the correct hidden sequence after the reveal, while pre-reveal accusation accuracy remains meaningfully below certainty. New-player groups should at minimum understand which parts of their own story were facts and which were deductions.

PART XVI - RESEARCH SOURCES AND DESIGN NOTES

Primary ferret appearance references:
American Ferret Association, Ferret Color and Pattern Standards:
https://www.ferret.org/events/colors/colorchart.html

American Ferret Association, Ferret Colors and Patterns PDF / guide:
https://www.ferret.org/pdfs/Ferret_Colors_and_Patterns.pdf

PetMD, Ferret Colors, Markings, and Patterns:
https://www.petmd.com/ferret/ferret-colors-markings-and-patterns

Relevant deduction-game references:
Ultimate Werewolf: Ultimate Edition rule summaries and role descriptions:
https://www.rulespal.com/ultimate-werewolf-ultimate-edition/rulebook

One Night Ultimate Werewolf: Daybreak rulebook:
https://www.rulespal.com/one-night-ultimate-werewolf-daybreak/rulebook

Daybreak Bodyguard / Dream Wolf reference:
https://www.ultraboardgames.com/one-night-ultimate-werewolf/daybreak-game-rules.php

Cheese Thief English rules PDF:
https://tesera.ru/images/items/2160806/CheeseThief_Rules.pdf

Cheese Thief publisher/product information:
https://www.jollythinkers.com/en/products/cheese-thief

Chupacabra reference used to understand the wolf-first target restriction:
https://gridbeast.gg/ultimate-werewolf-chupacabra/

The mechanical conclusions in Ferret Frenzy are original combinations and adaptations. The dossier does not reproduce source-game card text or artwork.

PART XVII - AUTHORITATIVE BASE-RULE SUMMARY

If another document, prototype, or older dossier contradicts the following list, this list wins.

ROLES
Exactly eight: Bandit, Dooker, Itchy, Trouble, Business, Snuggler, Hunter, Guardian.

DECK
Exactly twenty-four base cards:
4 Bandit
14 Business
1 Dooker
1 Itchy
1 Trouble
1 Snuggler
1 Hunter
1 Guardian

OBJECT
Bandit Raider steals the Minnow Treats.

NO LEADER
No Sheriff, Mayor, Captain, police role, leader, or tie-break authority.

DICE
/1/ selects d6.
/2/ selects d12.
Joystick flick rolls the selected die.
Raw rolls are private.

WAKE DIE
d12.
One result equals one Burrow Hour.
Multi-wake roles roll multiple distinct d12s.

ACTION DIE
d6.
Hunter conversion uses 1-2 as low.
Itchy special observation activates on 5-6.
Bandit Raider ties use private lowest-d6 tiebreak.

WAKE COUNTS
Bandit: 1 d12.
Dooker: 3 d12.
Itchy: 1 d12 normally, 2 if activated.
Trouble: 1 d12.
Business: 1 d12.
Snuggler: 1 d12.
Hunter: 2 d12.
Guardian: 1 d12.

DOOKER
One Glimpse and one informed Hammock Trip.

ITCHY
Partial clue only on activated nights.

TROUBLE
Blindly swaps two other players.
Becomes In On It when present for the theft.

BUSINESS
Ordinary role.
If alone during its wake, may inspect one other player's wake hour.

SNUGGLER
Creates a player-bound bond.

HUNTER
Knows Guardian initially.
Wakes twice.
If sharing an hour with a Bandit, rolls d6.
1-2 converts Hunter to Bandit side.
Guardian is not told.
Loyal Hunter can set one conditional Hunt Mark.

GUARDIAN
Knows Hunter initially.
Never learns Hunter conversion.
Protects one other player once per game during final vote resolution.

WIN
Business side wins by catching the historical Raider.
Bandit side wins if Raider escapes.
Catching Trouble accomplice or converted Hunter alone is not enough.

CARD MOVEMENT
Starting-role actions still occur.
Current cards can move.
Raider, Trouble conversion, Hunter conversion, and Snuggle Bond are player-bound statuses.

TIES
All unprotected players tied for highest Paw Point total are caught.
No leader breaks ties.
